First, let me recall what these elements are:
- **Destruction box (D-box)**: A consensus motif in proteins targeted for APC/C-mediated degradation. The consensus is typically: RxxL (arginine, then two variable positions, then leucine) or more specifically, the core consensus is often described as RxxL (at positions 1-4) with the full consensus being something like R-x-x-L-x-x-x-x-N or similar. The most commonly cited D-box consensus is: **RxxL** (Arg-X-X-Leu) where the Arg and Leu are critical, often with a glycine at position 1 or the full consensus being RxxL followed by additional residues.

Actually, the more precise destruction box consensus is: **R-x-x-L-x-x-x-x-N** (where R is at position 1 and L is at position 4, with N at position 9 in the extended form), or more commonly described as having the core motif **RxxL** with an additional **G** or other residues.

The classic D-box consensus from the literature (Glotzer et al., 1991) is: **RxxL** (positions 1 and 4 being Arg and Leu are critical). More specifically, the consensus is often given as **R-x-x-L-x-x-x-x-N** or the extended version.

- **KEN box**: A motif (K-E-N) that is also involved in APC/C-mediated degradation, particularly for substrates that bind Cdc20.

HES/HEY proteins:

- Function as transcriptional repressors
- Bind to N-box sequences (CAGCGTGG) in DNA
- Repress transcription of target genes
- Are involved in development, particularly neurogenesis and somitogenesis

- Negative regulation of DNA-templated transcription (B) - This is for repressors

The question asks to distinguish &quot;direct contribution from a related process that might only be affected downstream.&quot; This is important because:
